## [OrchBench: Isolating Multi-Agent Orchestration Evaluation via Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25656) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced OrchBench, a simulation-based benchmark that evaluates multi-agent orchestration plans in isolation using directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) and deterministic simulation, avoiding confounding factors from real execution. This benchmark provides an efficient and interpretable way to compare orchestration algorithms, reducing token costs to 1.3% and wall-clock time to 10.3% while maintaining high correlation with real execution quality (Pearson r=0.816). It enables systematic diagnosis of orchestration failures, such as coordination overhead diminishing parallelism benefits. OrchBench constructs DAGs from real-world tasks with controlled sizes and parallelism, and a deterministic simulator evaluates plans without invoking worker agents. The benchmark revealed that preserving task-critical information is more important than simply increasing the number of agents.

**Background**: Multi-agent systems decompose complex tasks into subtasks that may have dependencies, requiring orchestration to assign subtasks and manage information flow. Traditional end-to-end evaluations conflate orchestration quality with worker capabilities and environmental noise, making it hard to isolate orchestration performance. DAGs are a natural way to represent task dependencies, where nodes are subtasks and edges indicate dependencies.

## [OmniDelta: Training-Free Budget Allocation for Token Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25669) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OmniDelta is a training-free, skill-driven framework that couples intent-aware inter-modal allocation with content-aware intra-modal allocation for token compression in OmniLLMs. It constructs audio and video skill pools to shift budgets based on query demand and reallocates modality budgets using local complexity and temporal redundancy. This work addresses the underexplored budget-allocation problem in token compression for OmniLLMs, which is critical for reducing memory and inference costs. By establishing a new accuracy-efficiency Pareto frontier, OmniDelta enables more practical deployment of multimodal models. At 25% token retention on Qwen2.5-Omni-7B, OmniDelta reduces GPU memory by 22.0% and achieves a 1.64x end-to-end speedup over full-token inference. It is compatible with existing pruning strategies and preserves the total retained-token ratio.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Omni-modal Large Language Models (OmniLLMs) process text, audio, and video jointly, but long audio-video token sequences cause high memory and latency. Existing token compression methods focus on selecting important tokens under fixed budgets, ignoring how budgets should be allocated across modalities and within each modality. OmniDelta introduces a skill-driven approach to dynamically allocate budgets based on query intent and content complexity.

Researchers used LLMs to generate sustainability-focused recommendation explanations based on nudge theory, and validated them through human evaluation and LLM-as-a-judge audits in two studies (N=529) covering low-involvement (instant coffee) and high-involvement (hotel bookings) domains. This work demonstrates a scalable, theory-grounded method to nudge consumers toward eco-friendly choices without restricting options, addressing a key societal challenge of sustainable consumption. It also reveals a divergence between perception and behavior, where plain disclosure improves explanation evaluations but does not change actual choices. The study found that merely disclosing sustainability information in explanations did not change choices, but framing that information or invoking a descriptive social norm significantly increased sustainable selections and eased decision-making. The LLM-as-a-judge audit was used to validate explanation quality alongside human evaluation.

## [Bayesian Network Monitors Uncertainty in LLM Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25877) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Bayesian network-based method for runtime uncertainty monitoring in LLM-based multi-agent systems, applied to actuarial risk modeling. It uses token-level log-probabilities transformed into calibrated task-level confidence estimates to propagate uncertainty through the workflow. This work addresses a critical need for reliable uncertainty quantification in high-stakes actuarial applications, where unreliable LLM outputs can lead to incorrect risk assessment and regulatory non-compliance. It provides a principled way to monitor and propagate uncertainty in multi-agent workflows, enhancing trustworthiness. The framework uses length-normalized log-probability summaries transformed into calibrated task-level confidence estimates before incorporation into the Bayesian Network. Results show it reproduces baseline actuarial performance while providing additional insight into workflow stability and runtime uncertainty propagation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multi-agent systems for complex tasks, but their probabilistic nature introduces uncertainty that can be problematic in high-stakes domains like actuarial modeling. Bayesian networks are probabilistic graphical models that represent dependencies among variables and can propagate uncertainty through a system. Token-level log-probabilities from LLMs indicate the model's confidence in each generated token, but they are not directly interpretable as task-level confidence and require calibration.

Researchers propose DPR-GM, a framework that uses a large language model (LLM) to extract domain knowledge from system documentation and construct a domain-prior-regularized graph for anomaly detection in cyber-physical systems (CPS) with limited data. This approach addresses the critical challenge of scarce labeled anomalies in CPS by incorporating physical coupling knowledge, outperforming baselines on the SKAB benchmark. It demonstrates a practical alternative to fully learned graph topologies, potentially improving industrial monitoring reliability. The anomaly score is weighted by sensor reliability from the coefficient of variation, and all graph components are fixed prior to training with no learnable parameters.

## [Study Finds No Link Between Trust and Chatbot Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24761) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A mixed-methods survey of 153 frequent chatbot users found no detectable association between trust and verification of system outputs, challenging a long-held assumption in human-AI interaction research. This finding reframes user oversight as routine epistemic governance rather than trust-contingent behavior, with practical implications for designing chatbot interfaces that support verification without undermining trust. The study identified a substantive distinction between evaluative oversight (trust-decoupled, weakly tied to satisfaction) and interventionist oversight (weakly trust-correlated, strongly tied to satisfaction), and found a medium-to-large satisfaction-control gap.

**Background**: Previous HCI research often assumed that users verify AI outputs when they distrust the system, implying that better-calibrated trust would reduce verification. This study tested that assumption empirically and found no support for it. The concept of epistemic governance refers to frameworks for managing knowledge to guide decisions, which the authors propose as a new lens for understanding user oversight.

## [CKM: Prompt-Level State Enforcement Reduces LLM Inconsistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose the Cognitive Kernel Model (CKM), a prompt-level layer that forces LLMs to separate inputs into Fact, Heuristic, and Emotion roles before deciding, reducing behavioral inconsistency without changing model weights. LLM behavioral inconsistency undermines trust and reliability in applications like decision support and AI safety. CKM offers a lightweight, weight-free method to improve consistency, which could be widely adopted in production systems. CKM was evaluated on 26 LLMs from four vendors with 37,403 observations, reducing decision-flip rates by 82% in newer models (g=1.52). The effect is not due to JSON formatting alone, and the advantage grows under higher sampling temperatures (g=2.87 at temperature 0.7).

This approach does not modify the model's weights but rather enforces a structured thought process through the prompt.

Researchers introduce ToC-guided page retrieval for RAG, which infers headings from visual formatting without LLM calls, embeds them as a parallel index, and loads full page sections. In experiments across 1,280 conditions on 8 enterprise documents, this method significantly improves answer quality (d = +0.41) and contributes 20% of citations while adding only 2.9 pages per query. This work addresses a key limitation of chunking in RAG by preserving document structure without extra computational cost. It provides factorial evidence that document-side, query-side, and answer-side enhancements are complementary, offering a practical and efficient way to improve retrieval quality in production systems. The method outperforms query-side decomposition combined with verification (d = +0.32, p = 0.036). Gains are directionally larger on longer documents (up to +1.50 on 118 pages), and a 480-condition sensitivity analysis found no significant parameter effects, confirming defaults are near-optimal.

**Background**: RAG systems typically split documents into chunks, which destroys structural information like headings and sections. Existing heading-based retrieval methods require multiple LLM calls per document and return sub-chunks within matched sections. ToC-guided page retrieval infers headings from visual formatting (e.g., font size, boldness) without LLM calls, creating a parallel index that maps headings to full pages, thus preserving document structure and improving retrieval coherence.

The paper introduces TraceBound, a diagnostic protocol for adaptive knowledge-graph retrieval, and reveals that adding conditioning (query profiles and trace hints) consistently reduces retrieval quality under open-weight controllers. This finding challenges the common assumption that 'thinking before retrieval' always improves performance, and it reframes adaptive retrieval as a control problem over action selection rather than a prompt-format change. The degradation is localized to repeated calls, zero-result calls, and misallocated exploration budget.

## [Reliability-Aware Audit of Molecular Representations for Olfaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24848) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes a reliability-aware audit framework for evaluating molecular representations in human olfaction, showing that predictive accuracy alone is insufficient. The study compares MoLFormer and ChemBERTa against RDKit descriptors and Morgan fingerprints across multiple claims. This work challenges the common practice of relying solely on predictive accuracy to validate molecular representations, which is crucial for machine learning applications in scientific domains. It establishes empirical boundaries for generic molecular encoders and motivates a broader evaluation principle. Human three-attribute rating geometry showed median RSA of 0.743 and 0.855 across participant splits, while model-human alignment was much weaker (RSA 0.019-0.158). Learned embeddings did not consistently outperform conventional representations, and MoLFormer provided no clear incremental predictive value beyond a combined RDKit-Morgan baseline.

**Background**: Molecular representations like MoLFormer and ChemBERTa are pretrained on large chemical datasets to predict properties. Researchers propose DisasterTD, a framework that integrates multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with cross-view geolocalization to disambiguate vague geographic references in social media imagery for disaster response. The system achieves geolocalization accuracy of 71.62% within 1000 meters on the Hurricane Harvey dataset, significantly outperforming baselines. Accurate geolocalization of social media imagery is critical for timely disaster response, but vague toponyms often hinder this process. DisasterTD demonstrates that combining semantic reasoning from MLLMs with cross-view visual matching can dramatically improve localization precision, offering a practical solution for emergency management and geospatial AI. The framework first uses MLLMs to extract toponyms and generate candidate geolocations from noisy text, then applies cross-view matching between social media imagery, remote sensing imagery, and optionally street-view imagery to verify candidates. On the Hurricane Harvey dataset, the method reduces mean error to 11.33 km and median error to 0.68 km, with largest improvements for ambiguous toponyms.

This benchmark enables auditable evaluation of diagnostic models by revealing which plausible alternatives are ranked above the target and what evidence the model examines, addressing a critical gap in existing benchmarks that only report disease ranks. On a 237-case gene-component-disjoint test split, supervised rankers achieved MRRs from 0.640 to 0.740 and target-over-confounder accuracies from 0.898 to 0.916; agents using Agents-A1 and DeepSeek-V4-Flash achieved MRRs of 0.746 and 0.718, but their evidence coverage differed by 0.561.

## [GDRO: Generative Distributionally Robust Optimization Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24983) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Generative Distributionally Robust Optimization (GDRO) introduces a framework that uses any sampleable conditional generator as the nominal model and restricts worst-case distributions via Sinkhorn divergence, enabling finite-sample approximation and differentiable optimization. GDRO bridges generative models and distributionally robust optimization, addressing a key trade-off between model compatibility and adversarial structure, and demonstrates significant practical improvements such as 60% reduction in rare-context inventory regret and 50% reduction in navigation collisions. The framework uses a sampler-Sinkhorn pairing: samplers represent conditional laws exactly, while Sinkhorn divergence compares distributions without requiring likelihood access and can be estimated from samples alone. For Lipschitz losses, the population Sinkhorn radius bounds downstream degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Distributionally robust optimization (DRO) studies decision problems under uncertainty where the true probability distribution is ambiguous, using an ambiguity set of possible distributions. Generative models like GANs can produce samples from complex conditional distributions, but integrating them into DRO has been challenging due to compatibility issues. Sinkhorn divergence is a debiased entropic-regularized optimal transport cost that accurately compares probability measures and ensures zero self-similarity.

Researchers introduced CogArena, a procedurally generated 13-paradigm benchmark with a multimethod framework to evaluate whether cognitive-task scores in large language models form meaningful dimensional groupings. Across 55 open-weight models, they found limited evidence for selective grouping effects, with small within-grouping advantages that were scoring-sensitive and uncertain across model families. This work addresses a critical question in LLM evaluation: whether cognitive ability scores can be interpreted as stable, multidimensional profiles. The findings caution against overinterpreting per-ability labels and highlight the need for rigorous multimethod validation before attaching cognitive labels to model scores. The benchmark includes five theory-motivated groupings and uses a multimethod framework combining behavioral signatures, covariance analysis, matched interventions, and out-of-family prediction. A frozen confirmation study across 12 models from six families failed to show robust selectivity, and a post-hoc replication produced only a smaller positive estimate.

In medical imaging, such behavior can undermine model reliability and patient safety.

TabRank explores two distillation variants: explicit CoT distillation and conditioning the student reranker on the teacher's reasoning trace within the prompt. It is stress-tested on out-of-distribution generalization and multi-table scenarios, with code, data, and models publicly available.

Chain-of-thought distillation transfers reasoning capabilities from large teacher models to smaller student models, enabling efficient deployment.

## [RIDGE: Autonomous Validation Framework for LLM Option Pricing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25199) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced RIDGE, an autonomous framework that validates LLM-generated option pricing code through structured no-arbitrage tests, stress tests, benchmark comparisons, and consistency checks, with iterative refinement. The framework was applied to five stochastic volatility models, removing all detected defects and discovering two new semi-analytic pricing methods. This addresses a critical reliability gap in LLM-generated quantitative finance code, where numerical methods must be mathematically consistent and stable. The framework's ability to discover new pricing methodologies also demonstrates potential for advancing financial modeling beyond mere validation. RIDGE uses a repository to accumulate validation knowledge across models and iterations, enabling systematic refinement. The framework detected and removed all implementation defects in five stochastic volatility models, and in two cases, the validation process itself led to new semi-analytic pricing methodologies.

## [TopoGR Preserves Latent Topology of Semantic IDs in Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25216) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TopoGR introduces a topology-preserving generative recommendation framework using Bit-decomposable Semantic IDs (Binary SIDs) that expose Hamming geometry, enabling the model to capture item relatedness beyond exact SID matching. It outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on four benchmark datasets. This work addresses a fundamental structural mismatch in semantic ID-based generative recommendation, where item relatedness was previously limited to exact ID overlap. By preserving topology, TopoGR improves the identification of semantically similar items, potentially enhancing recommendation accuracy and user experience in large-scale systems. TopoGR uses Bit-decomposable Quantizer (BDQ) to learn Binary SIDs that can be deterministically converted to standard integer SIDs while preserving Hamming distance. The framework incorporates Hamming proximity at the input layer, Hamming soft targets for supervision, and Hamming-consistent reranking during inference.

**Background**: Generative recommendation models represent items as sequences of discrete semantic IDs (SIDs) learned from content, and predict the next item by generating SIDs. ## [SRPO: Structure-Aware RL for Better Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25268) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SRPO, a structure-aware relative policy optimization framework for listwise ranking that measures permutation differences using top-weighted Kendall-tau distance and normalizes reward differences accordingly. This addresses a key limitation in existing RL-based ranking methods that treat permutations as atomic outputs, leading to inaccurate credit assignment. SRPO could improve ranking effectiveness and stability in information retrieval and recommendation systems, especially under limited feedback. SRPO quantifies reward improvement per unit of ranking change, emphasizing efficient local refinements at top positions. Experiments show it improves effectiveness and stability in listwise ranking, particularly in limited-feedback and complex list-level optimization settings.

SRPO introduces structure awareness by using a top-weighted Kendall-tau distance to measure permutation similarity.

ScaleResfusion introduces Residual Rectified Flow (RRF), a diffusion framework that adapts pre-trained text-to-image rectified-flow models for real-world image restoration by learning a residual vector field from noisy low-quality images instead of starting from pure Gaussian noise. This approach significantly improves inference speed and fidelity compared to prior diffusion-based restoration methods, and enables parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large pre-trained models, making high-quality image restoration more practical and scalable. RRF maintains consistency with the pre-trained rectified-flow model's output distribution and linear diffusion process, allowing exact acceleration. A knowledge-distillation pipeline further reduces sampling cost while preserving restoration quality.

A new retraction-free and penalty-free optimization algorithm for Stiefel manifolds is proposed, with global convergence guarantees and best-known iteration complexities. It is applied to LoRA fine-tuning of large language models as Manifold-LoRA, achieving geometry-accelerated adaptation. This work eliminates the need for costly orthonormalization and careful penalty tuning in Stiefel manifold optimization, potentially making LoRA fine-tuning more efficient. It bridges manifold optimization and LLM adaptation, offering a principled approach to accelerate training while maintaining strong downstream performance. The algorithm leverages the strongly-convex-like property of the quadratic penalty function and the proximal smoothness of the Stiefel manifold. It achieves global convergence under both constant and diminishing step sizes with best-known iteration complexities.

Researchers introduced a physics-informed neural operator (PINO) that factorizes the Fourier neural operator into lateral and axial branches and uses background decomposition to accelerate 3D EUV mask simulation, achieving a mean absolute error of about 7e-3 for scattered intensity. This work significantly reduces computational cost for rigorous 3D EUV mask simulation, enabling faster design cycles in advanced semiconductor manufacturing. It addresses a critical bottleneck in EUV lithography as feature sizes continue to shrink. The PINO is trained on about 16,000 mask designs from LithoBench without precomputed EM field solutions, and it retains full-vector coupling between mask and multilayer response without finite-order Born approximation. Combined with spectral damping, the PINO warm-start initialization accelerates the background-decomposed PSFD solver on finer discretizations.

Physics-informed neural operators combine neural networks with physical equations to learn efficient surrogates for such simulations.

This paper investigates using Student's t likelihood distribution in Bayesian neural networks, showing it outperforms the standard Gaussian likelihood across regression tasks, independent of data distribution and network architecture. This work addresses model misspecification in Bayesian neural networks by challenging the default Gaussian likelihood assumption, potentially improving robustness and predictive performance in uncertainty estimation. Student's t likelihood yields better predictive performance and sometimes shorter training times compared to Gaussian, while remaining easy to implement. The study used standard multilayer perceptrons on artificial and real-world datasets.

**Background**: In Bayesian neural networks, variational inference optimizes the evidence lower bound (ELBO), which includes a likelihood distribution typically assumed to be Gaussian for computational convenience. Student's t distribution is a heavy-tailed alternative that can better capture outliers and robustly model uncertainty.

The paper introduces CRoMa (Cross-confounder Robustness Margin), a sample-resolved metric that directly compares distances to cross-confounder biological matches and same-confounder biological distractors, overcoming limitations of the count-based Robustness Index (RI). CRoMa provides a principled basis for robustness assessment and model selection in pathology foundation models, which are approaching clinical deployment but remain vulnerable to shortcut learning from non-biological variation across centers. CRoMa recasts robustness as a cohort-wide margin distribution rather than a single pooled score, revealing substantial within-model heterogeneity and enabling tail-aware analysis. The metric was evaluated on 20 tile-level and 4 slide-level encoders across multiple benchmarks.

## [DAV-Det: Decoupled Audio-Visual Detection for AIGC Forgeries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25543) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DAV-Det, a decoupled audio-visual detection system that independently models forensic evidence from each modality, achieving state-of-the-art results and ranking 1st in the General AIGC Audio-Video Detection Challenge at IJCAI-ECAI 2026 DDL 2.0 Workshop with a final score of 0.8460. This work challenges the common assumption that audio-visual forgeries always show cross-modal inconsistencies, offering a more robust decision-level fusion approach that improves detection in general scenes. It sets a new benchmark for AIGC audio-video detection, which is critical for combating AI-generated disinformation. The visual detector uses multi-granularity representations at global, patch, and segment levels, while the audio detector employs a gated temporal-spectral dual-branch architecture to capture acoustic artifacts. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI can now create realistic audio-visual content beyond human deepfakes, such as AI-generated videos of general scenes. Existing detection methods often rely on feature-level fusion, assuming that forgeries cause inconsistencies between audio and video, but this assumption may fail in general scenarios. DAV-Det addresses this by decoupling modalities and fusing decisions at the output level.

## [IRIS: Training-Free Entity Alignment via Frozen LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25579) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IRIS proposes a training-free framework that constructs reusable identity representations from frozen large language models (LLMs) for entity alignment across knowledge graphs, achieving state-of-the-art results on four benchmarks without pair-specific training or candidate-wise inference. This work addresses a key limitation of existing LLM-based entity alignment methods by decoupling representation construction from alignment, enabling efficient and scalable cross-KG matching without repeated LLM inference. It could significantly reduce computational cost and improve the practicality of entity alignment in large-scale knowledge graph integration. IRIS uses a frozen LLM to generate iris-like signatures for each entity, which are then directly compared across KGs via similarity measures. The best variants achieve Hits@1 scores of 100.00, 99.38, 98.31, and 97.99 on D-Y-15K V2, DBP-WIKI, ICEWS-WIKI, and ICEWS-YAGO, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Entity alignment (EA) is the task of identifying entities across different knowledge graphs that refer to the same real-world object. Traditional EA methods rely on graph structures and textual fields, but struggle with heterogeneous descriptions. LLMs offer deeper semantic understanding, but existing LLM-based methods often require pair-specific training or candidate-wise inference, limiting scalability. IRIS overcomes this by constructing a shared identity space from a frozen LLM, enabling direct similarity comparison without retraining.

## [BeyondUncertainty: Selective Retrieval via LLM Confidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BeyondUncertainty, a method that uses the LLM's own verbalized confidence to decide whether to retrieve external evidence, reducing computation while improving accuracy. It achieves 0.483 mean token-level F1 across six QA benchmarks, outperforming both always-retrieval and no-retrieval baselines. This work addresses the inefficiency of indiscriminate retrieval in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), offering a practical way to reduce computational cost and irrelevant evidence. It demonstrates that even poorly calibrated confidence scores can serve as a useful routing signal, opening up new possibilities for efficient LLM deployment. BeyondUncertainty uses a model-specific threshold selected on held-out validation data to decide whether to retrieve top-5 TF-IDF passages. While it reduces retrieved passages by 20.4% relative to always retrieval, the additional probe for confidence increases total token usage by 28.2%, revealing a trade-off.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by retrieving relevant external knowledge before generating answers, but it often retrieves indiscriminately, wasting computation and potentially introducing noise. Verbalized confidence is a technique where the LLM is prompted to output its own uncertainty estimate as part of the response, which can be used to gauge answer reliability without external calibration.

## [PI-BLS: Backpropagation-Free Physics-Informed Learning for PDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25608) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose PI-BLS, the first physics-informed broad learning system that solves partial differential equations without backpropagation by using a pseudoinverse-based linear optimization. This replaces the costly gradient-based training of traditional PINNs with a single deterministic least-squares step. PI-BLS significantly reduces training time and computational cost while maintaining competitive accuracy, addressing a key bottleneck of PINNs. This could accelerate scientific computing applications in engineering, physics, and other fields where PDEs are central. The framework embeds differential operators and boundary conditions into a linear output layer, solved via the Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse. Experiments on forward PDE benchmarks show PI-BLS achieves superior or comparable performance with fewer parameters and faster training than conventional PINNs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) embed physical laws into deep neural networks to solve PDEs, but they rely on iterative gradient-based optimization, which is slow and computationally expensive. Broad learning systems (BLS) offer an alternative by using random feature mapping and pseudoinverse-based learning, avoiding deep architectures and backpropagation. PI-BLS combines these ideas to create a physics-informed framework that is both efficient and scalable.

## [Dual-Domain Manifold Modeling for Hyperspectral Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25338) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new framework called Dual-Domain Manifold Modeling (DDMM) is proposed, which introduces a Topology-Aware Transformer (TPFormer) and a Frequency-Decoupled Spatial-Spectral Collaborative Fusion (FDSCF) module to improve hyperspectral image fusion by better modeling spatial and spectral geometric constraints. This work addresses key limitations in existing hyperspectral image fusion methods, such as weak spatial-spectral interaction and insufficient exploitation of spectral manifold structures, leading to superior spatial structure preservation and spectral reconstruction. It could advance applications in remote sensing, medical imaging, and other fields relying on high-quality hyperspectral data. The TPFormer combines global attention with neighborhood propagation to model spatial topology and pixel-level manifold relationships, while the FDSCF module uses discrete cosine transform to decouple features into low- and high-frequency components, selectively enhancing geometry-aware high-frequency features guided by a low-rank structural prior and spectral-driven spatial enhancement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperspectral image fusion aims to combine a low-resolution hyperspectral image (LR HSI) with a high-resolution multispectral image (HR MSI) to produce a fused image with both high spatial resolution and rich spectral information. Existing methods often struggle to model geometric constraints in both spatial and spectral domains, leading to structural degradation and poor spectral reconstruction. The proposed DDMM framework leverages manifold learning and transformer architectures to address these challenges.

Researchers propose a similarity-driven optimization framework that integrates multiscale spatial similarity as an objective and cartographic constraints to automatically determine parameter configurations for line generalization algorithms. This framework unifies similarity evaluation, constraint modeling, and parameter optimization into a single process, enabling more adaptive and interpretable automated cartographic generalization across scales. The framework was tested with multiple line simplification algorithms, target scales, and similarity measures including geometric, structural, and learning-based metrics, showing that combining similarity optimization with cartographic constraints yields more consistent and interpretable parameter control.

## [Set-Theoretic Formalization of al-Sabr wa al-Taqsim for Legal Cause Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25605) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a set-theoretic formalization of the classical usuli method of al-Sabr wa al-Taqsim (Examination and Division) for extracting legal causes ('ilal) from juristic verdicts within closed chapters of Islamic jurisprudence. A computational algorithm is introduced that computes minimal operational rules from a complete truth table of rulings. This work bridges classical Islamic jurisprudence with modern computational methods, potentially enabling automated extraction of legal causes from juristic texts. It could assist scholars in systematically identifying the operative causes behind rulings, enhancing consistency and transparency in legal reasoning. The algorithm requires a finite school-relative concept vocabulary and a complete ruling table for the closed chapter under investigation. The principal result is that the algorithm computes minimal structural generators of the ruling and eliminates all logically redundant attributes, yielding admissible candidate causes for juristic evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: In Islamic jurisprudence (usul al-fiqh), al-Sabr wa al-Taqsim is a method used to identify the effective cause ('illah) of a legal ruling by systematically examining and dividing possible attributes. The method is applied within closed chapters (e.g., specific topics like prayer or sales) where all relevant rulings are known. This paper formalizes that reasoning process using set theory and Boolean logic, enabling computational implementation.

<a id="item-230"></a>
## [Concept Bottleneck Models Can Hide Scale Without Losing Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25748) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study shows that concept bottleneck models for ejection fraction estimation can match direct regression accuracy even when the intermediate concept layer (ventricular volumes) lacks physical scale, revealing a fundamental invariance in the training objective. This finding challenges the common assumption that accurate concept prediction guarantees meaningful intermediate representations in clinical AI, highlighting the need to validate concept layers against the invariance structure of the loss function rather than just prediction accuracy. Without volume supervision, the predicted volumes collapsed to a spread of 0.1 mL (reference spread 35.7–45.7 mL), yet ejection fraction error remained low (6.89 vs 7.13 MAE). Adding volume supervision reduced volume error from 89.8 to 25.8 mL at a cost of only 0.4 in ejection fraction error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Concept bottleneck models (CBMs) are interpretable architectures that predict human-understandable concepts (e.g., ventricular volumes) before making a final prediction (e.g., ejection fraction). Ejection fraction is a ratio of stroke volume to end-diastolic volume, so scaling both volumes equally leaves the ratio unchanged. This invariance means the loss function cannot distinguish between physically correct volumes and arbitrarily scaled ones without additional supervision.

## [DocAnnot: GenAI-Powered Auto-Annotation for KIE Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24745) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced DocAnnot, a framework that uses a large vision-language model (LVLM) and a novel Spatially Informed Contextual Matching (SICM) algorithm to automatically annotate key information extraction (KIE) datasets, achieving F1-scores of 0.679 on CORD and 0.846 on SROIE. This work significantly reduces the manual effort required to create KIE training datasets, enabling faster prototyping and cost savings, especially for resource-constrained settings. While not yet matching human annotation quality, it brings automated annotation closer to practical use. DocAnnot combines OCR for text detection, an LVLM for label value extraction, and the SICM algorithm to improve label-value association using spatial and textual matching. Models fine-tuned on DocAnnot's outputs, such as LayoutLMv3, achieved an F1-score of 0.6765 on CORD, showing respectable but not yet human-level performance.

Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs) can understand both text and images, making them suitable for automating parts of this process. CORD and SROIE are standard benchmarks for receipt-based KIE.

Researchers propose DDSNet, a dual-domain symmetry-aware network that integrates translation-equivariant spectral filtering with a symmetry-induced structural prior to predict photonic crystal surface-emitting laser (PCSEL) properties more accurately than existing AI baselines. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in PCSEL design by providing a reliable neural surrogate that can efficiently explore the vast photonic crystal lattice design space, potentially accelerating the development of high-performance lasers for applications like LiDAR and optical communications. DDSNet outperforms existing AI baselines in property prediction and high-throughput screening, especially in structure-sensitive regions, and component masking analyses show it learns property-specific dependencies aligned with physical priors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Photonic crystal surface-emitting lasers (PCSELs) use a two-dimensional photonic crystal structure to form a laser resonator that emits perpendicular to the wafer surface. Coupled-wave theory (CWT) provides a physical framework for modeling PCSELs but is computationally expensive for large-scale design space exploration. Neural surrogates aim to accelerate this process, but existing models often fail to exploit spectral components and asymmetric structures that are key to device performance.

## [Extremal Chowla Sets in Finite Groups via Human-AI Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24847) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces extremal Chowla sets in finite groups, deriving exact formulas for their maximum size in cyclic and abelian groups, and a linear analogue for finite field extensions. The work was developed using Google DeepMind's Co-Scientist AI system in a human-AI collaboration. These results advance extremal combinatorics and group theory by providing precise characterizations of Chowla sets, with potential applications to additive combinatorics and number theory. The human-AI methodology also demonstrates a novel approach to mathematical research. For cyclic groups, the paper derives a divisor formula and characterizes integers n where C(Z/nZ) equals φ(n). It also proves that liminf and limsup of C(Z/nZ)/φ(n) are 1 and infinity respectively. For finite abelian groups, an explicit formula is given via invariant-factor decomposition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Chowla sets are subsets of a finite group where every element has order greater than the size of the subset. They were introduced by Hamidoune in 1996 in the context of additive combinatorics. The Co-Scientist system is a multi-agent AI built on Gemini 2.0 that assists scientists by generating and refining hypotheses.

A new arXiv preprint (2607.24880) argues that standard metrics are insufficient for evaluating tabular embeddings in similarity search and proposes a human preference aligned evaluation procedure, illustrated with a Product Lifecycle Management (PLM) use case. This work highlights a critical gap in evaluating task-agnostic tabular embeddings for real-world similarity search, where human judgment often differs from standard metrics. It could lead to more trustworthy and practical embedding evaluation in business systems like PLM. The PLM use case demonstrates how standard metrics can misalign with human expectations in similarity rankings.

## [A-STFGCN: Eliminating Propagation Delay in Traffic Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24885) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose the Attention-Based Spatial-Temporal Fusion Graph Convolution Network (A-STFGCN) to address propagation delay in traffic flow prediction. The model uses a spatial-temporal fusion block with a multi-head self-attention mechanism based on a mask matrix to remove propagation delay errors and capture both long-term and short-term temporal features. This work directly tackles the overlooked issue of propagation delay in graph convolution-based traffic prediction models, which can improve the accuracy and timeliness of traffic flow forecasting. Better traffic prediction can optimize transportation systems and urban mobility, benefiting city planners and commuters. The A-STFGCN model was evaluated on five real-world datasets and outperformed eight baseline methods in overall performance while maintaining good computation and data utilization efficiency. The model explicitly removes propagation delay errors during spatial-temporal feature extraction using a mask-based attention mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic flow prediction is crucial for intelligent transportation systems, and graph neural networks (GNNs) are commonly used to model spatial-temporal dependencies in traffic networks. However, existing GNN-based methods often assume instantaneous information propagation between nodes, ignoring real-world propagation delays (e.g., traffic flow takes time to travel from one sensor to another). This paper introduces a novel attention mechanism to explicitly account for and eliminate such delays.

## [Malware Latent Stability Analysis Under Feature Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24896) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a latent-stability analysis pipeline for malware representations under feature-space perturbations, comparing full EMBER features, PCA, VAE, Mandelbrot escape-time descriptors, and a PINNFlow module. It defines Latent Escape Divergence (LED) and PINNFlow-derived metrics to characterize latent movement. This work provides a new diagnostic framework for evaluating malware detectors beyond clean-sample metrics, helping researchers understand how representations behave under adversarial perturbations. It could improve robustness assessment in security-critical machine learning systems. Experiments on 600,000 EMBER PE feature vectors show full EMBER features achieve ROC AUC of 0.9962 and F1 of 0.9713, while PCA-64 achieves 0.9846 and 0.9347. The proposed VAE+Mandelbrot+PINNFlow representation does not outperform baselines for clean classification but offers diagnostic value under perturbation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 30, 04:00

**Background**: Static malware detectors are often evaluated using metrics like accuracy and F1, which ignore how representations behave under feature-space perturbations. EMBER is a benchmark dataset of static PE file features. Latent Escape Divergence (LED) measures changes in escape-time profiles under perturbation, and PINNFlow is a physics-informed neural network framework originally for flow inversion, here adapted for latent movement analysis.

## [LLM-Based Trace Ranking for Numerical Claim Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25069) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents two approaches for ranking LLM-generated reasoning traces and verifying numerical claims in English and Arabic for the CheckThat! 2026 task: an LLM-based verifier fine-tuned with LoRA and a lightweight TF-IDF reward model with handcrafted features. Automated numerical claim verification is crucial for fact-checking in domains like finance and science, and this work explores multilingual capabilities and compares LLM-based versus lightweight approaches, providing insights for practical deployment. The LLM-based approach outperformed the reward model on most metrics, especially Recall@5, while the reward model performed better on the Conflicting class. Sub-claim decomposition did not improve performance, and for Arabic, AraBERT outperformed the multilingual baseline.

**Background**: Numerical claim verification requires both language understanding and quantitative reasoning. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that adapts large language models without full retraining. Best-of-N selection improves reasoning by picking the best trace from multiple candidates. TF-IDF is a classic text weighting scheme used here with numeric and temporal features.
